## Current continuation — October 17, 2026, 15:52 EDT

The [authorized overhaul](performance-overhaul/execution.md) continues implementation/testing; the earlier stopped/source-analysis status below is historical. The latest 450-second observation averages 2.995×, with busy intervals around 2.04×/2.43×, a 10.2-second accepted walking confirmation, four busy walking refusals and Orin dead at closure. Fifteen residents sleep/wake; the saved history shows Orin died of hunger despite carrying food. A driver error path can strand an idle resident after a busy exception; a narrow repair now passes focused qualification, with no hunger-rule change and no exact failed-request ID available. Drawing averages 107.23 engine submissions/second with a 178 ms maximum gap. **Smooth 3× is not achieved.**

The current experimental world doubles the preceding tenfold work durations and samples ordinary movement at up to four metres, with tighter nearby-sense bounds. Production per game hour halves relative to the preceding pacing, and some notices/following/landing retries can occur later. A crossing-prediction lifetime repair preserves checked save/load sighting identities; adaptive publication retains 8 ms I/O yields and targets 32 ms uncontended complete updates, returning sooner for waiting ordinary operations. Exact bounded calculations survive saving; broader deferred-saving trials remain rejected for their recorded costs. The [current specification](performance-overhaul-feature-spec.md), [results](../verification/performance/overhaul-implementation.md) and [ranked gaps](../verification/performance/bottlenecks.md) own scope/evidence, including remaining implementation and qualification. These changed-workload observations do not pass ordinary acceptance.

**Technical architecture used:** the existing architecture and ownership contracts were used October 17, 2026, 15:52 EDT; no general architecture rewrite or acceptance pass is implied.

## Current feature and design reconciliation — October 16, 2026, 18:36 EDT

This existing project document holds the feature plan and its technical design. The original player goal remains smooth, sustained 3× with the expanded population and meaningful daily activity. The following decisions describe integrated behavior, rather than proposed experiments:

| What changed                                                                                                                                | Why and tradeoff                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Current owner                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Ordinary gathering, fiber preparation and cord making take ten times their former game time.                                                | Fewer completions require fewer decisions and saves. Production is slower and needs cost more per output. This changes the bundled world, not the engine's clock.                                                     | [Base-world pacing](../worlds/base/time.md#everyday-work-durations)                                                                  |
| Complete action choices are prepared when requested through Actions, an object, inventory, a recipe or held-item targeting.                 | Avoid repeatedly calculating unused menus. Automatic suggestions and the three pinned quick actions were removed; some interactions need an extra menu or fresh target check. Immediate ongoing-work controls remain. | [Action discovery](../architecture.md#action-discovery-and-player-preferences)                                                       |
| Time spent waiting for route preparation remains owed to the simulation.                                                                    | Needs, deadlines and travel catch up afterward. This preserves elapsed time but can make catch-up visible; it does not make route calculation faster.                                                                 | [Navigation preparation](../architecture.md#navigation-preparation)                                                                  |
| Shared preparation, bounded database-write grouping, command-result tree storage and cheaper immediate material reads remove repeated work. | They preserve current effects, private evidence and save acknowledgments. Their isolated gains do not establish full-game speed.                                                                                      | [Current performance behavior](../architecture.md#performance-critical-path), [storage](../performance/storage.md#storage-execution) |
| Browser diagnostics now have explicit trace/profile file limits and failure-message accounting.                                             | Oversized captures fail explicitly; fewer detailed error examples may be retained. This limits the measuring tool, not the game. Qualification is recorded separately.                                                | [Capture limits](../limits/observability.md#ob01--explicit-browser-performance-capture)                                              |

The newer world-editing library, revised recent-result layout, slower idle-animal reviews, compact SQL transport, grouped resident decisions and alternative database indexes remain private trials. They are not part of the normally launched game. The [standalone reader report](../verification/performance/bottlenecks.md) distinguishes their measured results and compromises. Collision, animal activity and character needs have not been removed from the integrated game to claim success.

### Whole-category removal experiments

On October 15 Mike explicitly asks for decisive temporary removal of whole categories, including changes that would be unacceptable to retain. PF16.125 first measures the previously exact completed-world handoff with the unchanged counterless native driver, current canonical rules, 2,400 actual game seconds, the same 80 admission anchors, fourteen player walks and sixteen residents' actual produced-food/physical-return checks. Use B/C/C/B order and original 60-second in-loop/90-second outer bounds, no inspector, counters, trace serialization or forced collection. Reuse exact static/worker/native proofs; this is a new timing measurement, not SQL acceptance. All source, input, dependency and sidecar identities remain guarded. Native speed cannot establish the full game's speed.

Next prepare two separate diagnostic copies of the unchanged baseline: freeze all forty animals by excluding them from native simulation participants while keeping their objects visible, and skip the incremental sight/contact/recognition update. These intentionally remove gameplay and its coupled downstream work. The animal removal loses movement, animal physiology and native reactions; the noticing removal loses newly noticed people/objects and updated recognition, while direct command admission still uses its existing checks. Estimate 2–12 product logic lines per removal and 50–100 orchestration lines using existing fixtures and guards; source review precedes execution. Compare matching starting data and requested player/resident activity, actual progress, process CPU, publication count, outcomes, surviving population and changed events. Any incomplete/refused routine is evidence of the diagnostic's limits, never silently repaired. Repeat in alternating order. A combined removal is selected only after individual results identify an interaction. No production compromise is selected by these upper-bound experiments.

The first six removal runs establish a material animal contribution: freezing all40animals reduces mean total CPU49.806%, while suppressing incremental noticing reduces14.582% in its separate version. Both retain all resident/player activity gates; repeats match within version. Animal publications fall223→170; stopped noticing retains223, so their effects differ and are not additive. The measured population contains one flight-route bird and39other animals. Before further executable edits, select separate private native-participant filters for the one bird and the other39, each one expression, preserving the same driver/input/bounds and balanced B/bird/ground/ground/bird/B order. Only direct measured contribution will justify a production compromise.

PF16.126 now selects one count-only diagnostic per previously timed version. A10–20-line wrapper installs/removes the existing domain counter sink around the unchanged activity driver and writes an exclusive separate sidecar. Compare all normal activity gates and exact final digest with each prior version; instrumented CPU is not a timing result. Counts distinguish shared motion/forecast/rate/publication work from sight/crossing work. No new product instrumentation, SQL or provider is introduced.

The requested reporting reorganization also proceeds: preserve all existing evidence in smaller files under `docs/verification/performance/`, repair incoming links, retain the old report as an index, and maintain one standalone plain-language ranked bottleneck report. The ranked report is a living account until implementation possibilities have been pursued; a document is not completion of the still-unmet 3× target.

The [plain-language experiment report](../verification/smooth-game-experiments.md) is the reader-facing account. It starts with the actual outcome, before/after tables and gameplay compromises; the older detailed report retains source and clock evidence. PF16 remains the single experiment register. Before each attempt, record its hypothesis, affected behavior, expected scope, comparison and rejection rule there. Record failed and removed trials too.

The next implementation sequence is:

1. Separate time waiting to enter the world-change queue, native calculation, saving, screen-update preparation and discarded elapsed time. Use short comparisons on one fresh complete expanded opening before another long soak. Estimated diagnostic scope: 70–150 logic lines in existing tooling; no new measurement service.
2. Test packed reusable cape coordinates, then a simpler animated cape if necessary. The first candidate preserves physics and is about 180–240 logic lines; a simpler cape explicitly trades simulated folds/contact for lower cost. Compare exact motion when claiming equivalence, allocation supply, CPU and actual frames.
3. Measure the maximum possible benefit from removing routine physiology in a private diagnostic. Only if material, implement complete periodic meter/status ownership rather than a partial stale-value shortcut. Expected 400–650 logic lines across status, time, save validation and readers. A one-minute meter cadence and constant awake energy cost are authorized candidates, not yet retained behavior.
4. Measure complete action-menu preparation on demand and coarser physical/sensory updates if these remain dominant. The complete action-consumer change is now estimated at 700–1,000 logic lines after tracing the redesigned UI and equipped-item targeting; native cadence scope depends on the preceding measured bound. Record loss of immediate offers, observation precision or collision precision before selecting any compromise.
5. Reprofile after each retained change. Run one timed workload at a time; parallel agents may read, implement independent owners and review. Keep source identities frozen during matched measurements. Stop pursuing neutral changes and tackle the largest remaining measured cause.

Current findings narrow the next sequence. Packed cape storage is retained after exact-motion, isolated CPU and actual-browser allocation checks; frame tails remain mixed. Longer eight-cycle commitments improve no-view progress by only 6.9% in one pair, so no new production routine is selected. Removing all shared rate/status processing privately improves complete-server progress by 20.5%, still far below the target; no physiology or collision compromise is selected. Tenfold world-owned work durations are selected after native continuation/cancellation/needs checks: real-SQL progress improves from 0.656× to 2.917× without recurring views and reaches 2.832× with the ordinary viewer. This deliberately reduces production per game hour and increases time spent working per output; the changed shared cordage family also makes older base-world saves incompatible with current exact checks. Existing development data was not reset. The unopened-panel trial failed its allocation/frame gate and was removed. The memory-write candidate improves both ordered comparisons, reaching 2.939× and 2.929× with ordinary views; its small-sample gain does not establish full-game 3×. The combined graphical build reaches only 2.124×, with first displayed movement as slow as 1.225 seconds. Action preparation accounts for 79% of measured recurring view preparation before the menu change. The next gates qualify the complete on-demand menu implementation, measure its full-game benefit and establish actual resident travel/rest.

PF16.72 selects a bounded memory-persistence trial (~100–210 logic lines). Cost currently grows with new perceived events × observers and writes new lookup records twice. Preserve archive/delete preparation before canonical record replacement; then insert each family in dependency order with current eligibility computed by the existing SQL policy, and run the existing final dependent reconciliation. Share parameter/byte batching and retain only the current change references until insertion. Canonical forgetting/correction, awareness existence, summary links, source family, revisions and transaction/timeline boundaries remain dependencies; no cached permission or gameplay loss is introduced. Completion requires same-transaction/privacy/current-format/rollback qualification and a matched actual-server improvement, followed by whole-game remeasurement; reject a neutral candidate and record the result. These functional gates and both ordered short comparisons now pass. Retain the three-owner change and include the moved insertion work in the existing stress measurement; complete combined static/build and graphical/sustained qualification before closing the project.

### Assessing simulation cadence and animal activity

Subsequent source verification finds a required constraint for the proposed 0.03 m/game-second bird option: current validation requires climb speed no greater than route speed. The unchanged 0.06 climb setting would make that proposal invalid. Any selected 0.03 trial must also set the authored climb speed to 0.03. The earlier one-scalar description below is incomplete for that option; no invalid candidate has been created or run.

PF16.105 selects source-only architectural assessment while the separately qualified trials run. The current aged CPU groups include3.28sampled seconds coordinating progression,2.31updating needs and1.53determining encounters; no existing evidence assigns all of these to animals. Forty moving animals can generate motion boundaries, changed positions and observations even while people perform longer work. First trace native intervals, rate materialization/deadlines, current preparation/cache ownership and authored wandering/rest/threat behavior, reusing existing recorded counts only. Identify mechanisms already implemented rather than proposing them again. Compare: dependency-correct reuse within one working draft; exact deadline-driven needs updates; or more ordinary grazing pauses that retain immediate threats/actions. Assess viewer/relevance-based frequency only with all character observers and collision dependencies considered. Camera distance alone cannot establish that a living character has no meaningful work.

This follows primary engine research, not a claim that another engine’s mechanism or benchmark transfers. [Unreal MassGameplay](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/overview-of-mass-gameplay-in-unreal-engine) separates representation, simulation and per-viewer replication detail and permits grouped variable-frequency simulation. [Unreal Significance Manager](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/significance-manager-in-unreal-engine?lang=en-US) provides application-defined importance and budgets, but does not itself make a game faster. Our inference is to separate which calculation is needed from how often unchanged preparation is repeated, while treating any reduced activity/fidelity as an explicit gameplay compromise.

Completion of this assessment means current semantic owners/cost-growth dimensions, reuse/invalidation, viable scope estimates and behavioral/save/knowledge/randomness consequences are recorded, with a concrete measurement gate for any proposed trial. No runtime/tooling code, new profiler/game capture, cache framework, skipped clock time, dormant-character removal or canonical behavior change is authorized by this assessment alone. The prior2m and disabled-physiology diagnostics, negative read-only candidates and negative native batching result remain visible; no promised saving is inferred from sampled or allocation weights.

The source assessment is complete; no candidate is selected. The forty-animal fixture contains thirty-eight ordinary ground wanderers, one territorial stag and one routed bird. Ordinary undisturbed ground animals move0.4metres, then wait120–239game seconds; they are not forty continuously travelling bodies. Attack/flee/flight/fall and sufficiently small nearby sensing ranges retain their own exact/current motion bounds. The ordinary animal countdown and participation loops still visit the roster, but the existing profiles do not isolate their total cost. Longer grazing pauses alone would not remove bird/stag or character/action/need deadlines.

Much of the proposed exact/event-driven architecture already exists. One forecast retains selected status rates and participant lists through motion-only slices and, with strict identity, across its exact frozen native successor. Linear need updates are deferred within that forecast, then materialized before affected reads, effects, a new forecast or publication. Health and periodic effects remain immediate; exact concerns/thresholds, action completion, depletion, daily conditions, lifetimes and native intentions bound advancement. A flyer's isolated landing/takeoff can refresh only that participant when cross-character references permit it. Commands, edits, restore/fork identity, body/contribution/participation/attribute/behavior/module changes and unknown dependency changes require existing reconstruction. This is not an absent switch that can simply be enabled.

At the requested3× clock,15/30/60game seconds are0.083/0.167/0.333real seconds; the additional idle-detection delay bounds are0.083or0.250real seconds. **Behavior cost.** On horizontal legs the bird's speed becomes one-quarter current; pure vertical motion becomes half current because the old climb cap was already0.06. The whole lap is not exactly four times longer because vertical legs and the unchanged180g wait contribute differently. A one-metre horizontal path takes8.333→33.333game seconds, equivalent to0.046→0.185real seconds only if the requested3× clock is sustained. Idle checks15→60g can delay first unprovoked detection by up to45g beyond current (0.25real seconds at sustained3×); a brief entry/exit entirely between checks can be missed, not merely noticed late. Actual injury must still request a review at the current game instant and supply no unseen attacker identity/location. Once active, pursuit/search/return, attack timing and withdrawal keep their current schedules. Changed movement/detection may alter later actions, observations, discoveries and which animal consumes the next shared random number; cross-policy identical behavior/RNG is not an acceptance requirement or claim.

**Which work could change.** The idle stag's review is a genuine shared mechanics deadline. The bird's one-metre limits are private motion slices that can retain prepared rates; the kernel also counts a shared interval after32private slices. WorldService can nevertheless publish at an existing private boundary after its8ms responsiveness budget. Therefore slowing the bird is neither automatically four times fewer full-world finishes nor irrelevant to publication count. Retain separate actual motion-slice, shared-interval, native-call/publication and forecast counts in an untimed diagnostic. When both animals are quiet, the existing60g horizon still caps forecasts. During flight the nominal1m bound is33.333g; small nearby senses, waypoint/landing/blocked landing, falling, walking/approach/Follow, projectiles, weather, status/health, work completion and other real deadlines can shorten it. Active stag behavior still supplies15g. No unconditional15g global floor remains from the idle stag, but the expanded scene may still stop at15g or much less for other reasons. Measure those actual counts, not a source-derived speed promise.

### Scheduling-only forecast preparation assessment

PF16.110 is registered as a source-only assessment before a candidate. The earlier cadence assessment identified a possible180–300-line separation inside current forecast continuation. Determine whether a review that merely schedules its next check invalidates unchanged participant/rate preparation, and what preparation the existing owner already retains. Follow the actual kernel, forecast preparation, continuation identity, mechanical revisions and calculation-allowance consumers. Attribute source work to actual callers; do not treat overlapping CPU groups or allocation counts as removable time. Keep effects, exact condition/health/resource/action deadlines, current body and position reads, perception, randomness and command interposition intact. Mutable/draft edits, publication, fork/restore and unknown writes must invalidate correctly. Completion is a concrete source-backed scope and same-behavior proof gate or rejection; no new cache, candidate, runtime or speed claim is selected.

Initial source finding: an admitted `territorialIntention` always schedules its next review and records a behavior change, including an idle review finding no target. The kernel materializes pending values and unconditionally discards its current mechanics after that review. Its single `nativeInterval` also includes the next threat-review deadline in the same minimum as status predicates, meter thresholds, work, motion and other deadlines. At an interval end the kernel discards the complete preparation. Consequently, deleting that unconditional discard or ignoring the behavior revision alone would neither make expired preparation valid nor safely remove the repeated work.

Existing reuse is substantial: only an exact frozen native successor inherits the current preparation; motion-only slices defer linear rates; supported flight changes can re-predict one participant; status predicates and thresholds bound the interval; publication materializes every pending value. Commands, load, forks outside that native identity and unknown mutations rebuild. `prepareStatusRates` chooses actual active effect episodes, targets, conditions and numeric operations. `integrateStatusRates` reads current episodes and owns saved elapsed progress/periodic accounting. Ordinary numeric integration itself records attribute changes. A new certificate cannot classify every attribute revision as an external mutation, or ignore every attribute revision as harmless.

The plausible complete change is two derived validity horizons inside this existing owner: one for the next required discrete review/publication and one for unchanged selected rate/participant preparation. Calculate the latter from actual status/meter/daily/periodic/lifetime bounds, retain only proven linear preparation after known integration, and keep every current discrete boundary. Explicit action/support/body/participation/installed-rule changes, real status transitions, damage, cross-target effects, flight re-prediction and unclassified writes still invalidate or refine through their existing owners. Preserve normal behavior-change reporting for consumers; do not hide the review from dependency tracking. Current finite status fields do not read threat-mode metadata, but they do read whether an action is active, whether a body is grounded, current attributes, life, control and contribution state. An animal review that cancels or starts an action therefore is not a scheduling-only case.

The initial180–300-line idea is provisionally250–400logic lines after tracing integration and continuation: split temporal preparation/bounds, carry an independently expiring derived preparation through the existing kernel/weak continuation, and make invalidation explicit at owned phase boundaries. No new persisted clock, alternate writable world, saved rate, broad revision exception or world-specific optimization belongs in it. Exact publication times and arithmetic order should remain unchanged. Required evidence would include the existing complete239-transition comparison, ordinary15g idle reviews, active target/strike/search/return and injury, silent action start, all relevant status/rate/health/clamp/daily/cross-target cases, partial publication, mutable edits and fork/Save/Load reconstruction. Count full forecasts, selected-rate preparations, integrations, motion/shared intervals and publications separately in an untimed proof, then require counters-off native repeats and actual aged-service/input gains. Lower counters alone cannot retain it. Assessment remains open pending a concrete bounded preparation design or a demonstrated reason to reject it; no implementation or measured saving follows from these source findings.

### Longer needs-aware plan assessment

PF16.109 is a source-only assessment registered before executable changes. The earlier eight-cycle proposal was left unselected with a small improvement and delayed rest; those results remain controlling history, not evidence of a current gain. The current complete-service recordings still include substantial decision/command/save traffic. Determine whether the existing repeated-activity mechanism can retain a useful routine across meals while ordinary needs, changed goals, lost resources, observation and interruptions remain timely. A person can continue an already chosen useful task without generating a fresh plan after every meal, but fewer reconsiderations are a real cognition tradeoff that must be reported.

Read the existing composed diagnostic routine and actual native activity/review/continuation owners, not just its fixture schedule. Trace actual durable decision creation, plan admission and terminal reporting to distinguish real product costs from diagnostic choices. Preserve authoritative current action offers, finite physical resources, actual output bindings and distinct execution/history identities; the rejected receipt deletion is not a shortcut. Find whether installed need concerns can yield for normal Sleep between completed cycles without waiting for a fixed long sequence. Check reactive interruption and private character knowledge, saved repetition progress, cancellation, restart and failure. Do not invent a loop or new world law before this assessment identifies a missing consumed seam.

Completion is a source-backed decision on viability, approximate complete logic scope, real behavioral/save/identity/knowledge costs and a concrete measured gate, or rejection with its general cause. Any later trial must preserve all sixteen people's actual work, travel, food, return and ordinary rest at current durations, and count actual plans/commits/physical work separately. First compare it alone on the current baseline; combining it with longer durations or animal cadence would obscure the result. No code, ad hoc parser, profiler, game, paid inference or candidate is selected by this assessment.

**Assessment complete; no new candidate selected.** `scripts/performance/routine-plan.ts` implements the earlier eight-cycle trial as forty individual native actions in one fixed sequence. Its driver considers Sleep only after that sequence completes. One selection performs two separate diagnostic job-record writes and one authoritative world transition; these are not three world commits. The real director also persists readiness, performs action grounding and responds to changed needs, goals, possessions and observations. Neither the earlier no-view improvement (0.61465×→0.65699×) nor current overlapping save totals establishes what a longer plan would save in current ordinary play.

The activity engine already supplies finite sequences, conditional branches, repeats, current native admission and saved execution progress. Each executed step receives a new serial identity, and later actions can consume the actual output of an earlier action. It does not currently install a condition for the acting person's energy: the installed custom condition concerns observed fire fuel. Reaching a repeat's maximum without satisfying its stopping condition blocks that activity; simply increasing the existing repeat limit would not provide normal completion or timely rest. A possible future composition is eight conditional five-action cycles, checking the person's current installed energy concern between meals and skipping remaining cycles when rest is needed. It occupies57nodes within the current64-node bound and can preserve the current between-cycle rest opportunity. This is a proposed use of existing branches, not an implemented routine or a proof that other interruptions remain timely.

An energy condition alone would not expose this useful choice to actual people. Production proposals pass through `action-grounding.ts` and `typed-actions.ts`, while authored activity requests and learned activities supply discoverable supported choices. Typed sequences currently accept at most four clauses and do not compile arbitrary conditional work cycles. A complete trial therefore needs a world-owned self-energy condition, trusted installation/pinning, an explicit discoverable and bounded activity request or a genuinely learned method, ordinary grounding/projection, and corresponding diagnostic cycle accounting. It must not create a hidden canned invention or merely force a longer profiler plan. Provisional complete scope is180–300logic lines across world activity support, its actual server consumers and the existing diagnostic owners; uncertainty is in the player/NPC choice and learned-method boundary, not the basic branch executor. No new save schema, global receipt deletion, private target-energy disclosure or universal engine energy law is needed.

The compromise remains fewer voluntary chances to change a useful routine, even with between-meal rest checks. Ordinary resource/target validation, damage, body restrictions and current reactive director opportunities must remain; a conditional plan cannot promise a character notices an event it has not observed. Any future selection needs direct branch/unknown-condition/energy-crossing/output/cancel/interrupt/Save/Load proof, actual discoverability and grounding with no paid model-quality claim, and corrected observation of partly completed commitments. The existing travel fixture explicitly rejects composed routines, so that integration and its own-food/return/rest accounting must be qualified rather than toggled silently. A matched current full-service comparison must record all sixteen people's actual actions, food, travel, rest, plans, job writes, world commits, player input and elapsed game progress. The modest historical result and this additional product scope leave this option unselected while the already prepared duration and animal-frequency trials are measured. Assessment completion does not close the overall3× goal.

### Sharing finishing work across native intervals

**Completed experiment — rejected for now.** The private four-interval/32ms policy supplies no useful measured gain and is not installed. Fresh counterless native ABBA is 4.64% worse in native CPU and 7.16% worse in elapsed time. The separately qualified aged HTTP/SSE comparison also advances less game time: 1.18603× baseline versus 0.98445× candidate, with command p95 1392.40→1460.21ms. Candidate CPU falls with 16.67% less actual game progress; CPU per advanced game second rises 3.33%. Host load changes substantially, so this ordered pair cannot assign a causal slowdown to the policy. It establishes no benefit sufficient to retain the change. No further max4/32ms test, canonical integration or capacity claim is selected; the overall 3× goal remains open. The staged decisions and failures below are retained as the experiment's chronology, superseded by this disposition.

### Distance-based character presentation assessment

PF16.107 was registered before any executable candidate. The source-only assessment is complete; no representation rule, cache, frame limit or diagnostic is selected. The user permits visual compromises, while every authoritative person/animal, permitted visibility, knowledge, action and selectable interaction must remain intact. Only presentation is in scope; camera position must not prune simulation or grant perception.

#### What actually draws now

The current expanded workload has seventeen people and forty animals, plus133other roots; this is the complete fixture population, not a count of simultaneously visible/drawn entries. Only the bundled Mercenary appearance has an animated imported model and simulated cape. The other sixteen people and all forty animals use camera-facing image cards. Those cards already retain movement/attack image frames, current pose, alpha picking and lighting. Their ordinary casting geometry is a shared shallow volume derived from image transparency, rather than a two-triangle sheet. Sleeping rotates the same image/relief onto its real ground footprint; it does not currently choose cheap flat geometry. Separate reveal/protection copies already use a front plane.

`scene.ts` owns each permitted object's root, sprite, model visibility, interpolation, selection, level filtering, opacity and picking. Its `updateModelVisibility` already chooses model versus fallback for loading/failure, unobserved/hidden roots, horizontal/dead/removed states and unsupported action animation. `setSpriteRelief` switches shared texture-owned geometry when an image frame changes. These are useful local seams, but there is no general distance/detail policy. The Mercenary owner retains one fixed128×192 color/depth rendering pass, model/cape shadow casters and composite/reveal/foreground layers while loaded. Hiding it stops its wanted model/cloth preparation and drawing through existing visibility controls, but retains those assets. Returning requests a cloth reset and sixty settling steps, at most three per callback, while the fallback remains visible. The result can take twenty preparation callbacks and is not immediate detailed drawing or motion equivalence.

#### One coherent visual option, if selected later

Estimate100–160logic lines chiefly at the existing scene owner: one derived rich/flat decision shared by model availability and image geometry, one retained scene-owned front plane with explicit reference lifetime, and current-input invalidation. Rich means the existing supported model when available, otherwise the existing image relief; flat means that same current image/material on two triangles. Keep its real horizontal pose, movement/attack frames, alpha, dimensions/contact padding, public position/support, lighting, read-through and complete interactions. Do not use a sleeping pose for a far awake character. Retain model assets and existing texture/relief caches rather than adding eviction/reload policy or another importer.

A concrete prospective rule should use projected body height rather than raw distance from the camera: orthographic zoom changes apparent size without moving its camera nearer. For a future controlled trial, illustrative thresholds are promotion at64CSS pixels and demotion below48, keeping the previous choice between them. These are unselected visual-policy values. Use the current public dimensions and camera projection/viewport, with the controlled player and current identifiable selected object always requesting rich presentation. Existing model eligibility still wins: selecting a sleeping or unsupported-action Mercenary must not force an upright animated body. Apply current membership/privacy and floor visibility before either choice; an unseen remembered image cannot be promoted into live model animation. Re-evaluate on actual displayed-foot motion, camera pan/orbit/zoom/projection/follow/level/resize, selection, image/pose/appearance replacement and asynchronous model readiness. Initial construction and selection must use the same rule; no per-species/character exception or separate public endpoint is needed.

Changing ordinary relief to a plane does not remove the object's ordinary color submission, reveal/protection submission or the shared shadow preparation scan. It reduces geometry processed in its color/shadow passes; exact shadow grouping may change, and no draw-count reduction is promised. Disabling the one current Mercenary can remove its model/cloth work, geometry shadow submissions and offscreen character pass while adding its normal fallback path. No measured current CPU/GPU share establishes the saving. Far silhouettes retain their original artwork, but volume, close occlusion/depth, edge-on-light shadows, model-specific appearance/body animation and fabric motion are explicit losses. Promotion/demotion may visibly pop; repeated promotion restarts existing cloth settling. Current selection, pointer alpha/depth picking and delayed hover must track whichever representation is actually displayed, including during that settling delay. There is no new knowledge or action availability authority.

#### Existing reuse and the remaining common preparation

The scene does not redo every image operation every callback. It changes sprite materials/relief only when the image frame changes. It reorients cards for camera yaw/pitch, changed horizontal pose/heading, facing, movement/attack or geometry changes; stationary unchanged cards already avoid that work. Hidden read-through also has an early exit. Every observed entry still incurs current-foot cloning/comparison and opacity writes, while fades, interpolation, active animation, reveal easing, status/caption expiry and stationary-pointer rechecking have legitimate continuing dependencies. A cached whole entity based only on unchanged public position would incorrectly freeze those behaviors.

The shared `SpriteShadowBatches.sync` still visits all registered casting-capable cards, including decorative scenery and other image objects outside the person/animal proposal. Each callback clears/rebuilds group membership and string keys from current mesh/material/mask/cascade/opacity/spatial cell, then copies each member's transform/foot/size into21Float32values and recomputes group bounds. PF16.44 already skips an identical GPU upload and identical bounds setter after that preparation. Its actual96-state proof and isolated stationary5.35%CPU reduction, moving-neutral result and publication counts are retained in the [shadow evidence](../verification/performance/03-saving-and-render-preparation.md#unchanged-sprite-shadow-publication); they do not qualify skipping preparation. Existing component enable/disable can re-add individual shadow casters between refreshes, so caster-registration reconciliation must remain even when prepared bytes are reused.

A whole-batch unchanged-input fast path would be about90–140logic lines across the scene and shadow owner, but one continuously moving caster can invalidate it every callback. More useful per-affected-group reuse is provisionally140–220logic lines, not a bundle of tiny submodule fixes: scene-owned changes identify changed cards; the existing shadow owner retains each card's derived membership/packed inputs and recomputes only affected groups while preserving current member order, bounds and caster-registration reconciliation. Dependencies include creation/removal, root/card visibility and observation, model/fallback changes, material/image/relief, transforms/facing/horizontal pose, opacity, foot/size, masks/cascade settings, group-cell crossings, material release, geometry replacement and graphics recovery. Unknown external changes must fall back to a full refresh. Moving many cards can still require most preparation; dirty-group frequency is unmeasured. No internal PlayCanvas transform counter should become an unchecked cache key, and this would remain one derived presentation owner rather than another world representation.

It must establish material time in shared shadow preparation for the reuse option, or material model/relief drawing cost for the visual option; GPU cost cannot be inferred from CPU allocation. No such capture is launched by this assessment.

Any later implementation needs exact old/new prepared buffer/member-order/bounds/caster-registration comparison across the existing96cases and relevant new invalidations, then actual WebGL recovery/teardown. The visual option additionally needs both projections, orbit/zoom/pan/follow/floors/resize, selected and hovered threshold crossings, loading/failure/late replacement, actual Sleep/Wake/death/action fallback and paused settling. Ordinary click, context menu, keyboard/list selection and equipped targeting must keep the exact currently permitted entity and complete action routes; unseen/remembered/hidden targets remain refused. Check status/speech clocks and privacy/scope/timeline replacement without relying on another simulation tick. A matched sampler-free frame/input/progress comparison and a separate allocation comparison must justify any retained complexity or visual loss. The original eight insufficient-buffer warnings and sustained smooth3× failure remain open. Source assessment adds no provider calls; additional and cumulative task cost remain$0.

Each actual cloth step already samples the fifteen live capsule endpoints and their normalized directions once. Add six bounds to that same step-owned preparation. For nonnegative finite s, compute u=RN(s+1), then E(s)=RN(u+u×Number.EPSILON), where RN denotes the existing binary64 rounding. Since u≥1, scaling by2^-52 is exact and normal, and the increment is at least one ULP of u. The final finite result advances u by at least one representable value, enclosing exact s+1 even if the first addition rounded down. For each normalized direction d, use lower=-E(max(0,-d)), upper=E(max(0,d)). Any overflow/nonfinite bound makes that capsule ineligible for rejection until the next live preparation; no arbitrary tolerance or hidden clamping is introduced.

For every current particle/capsule pair, compute all three original projected coordinates in the original order. Only finite projections and valid bounds may reject a point strictly outside at least one axis. Every survivor and uncertain/nonfinite case executes the unchanged original projection-to-segment, residual, squared-distance and pushout expressions. A clamped finite segment parameter lies in[0,1], so its rounded coordinate product remains between0 and the corresponding finite endpoint. A successful original squared-distance test below1 requires every rounded residual magnitude below1; monotonic rounding cannot hide an exact coordinate difference of magnitude≥1. Therefore the expanded bounds contain every original successful contact. This derivation uses the same existing linear normalized coordinates for endpoints and points; individually normalized PlayCanvas basis directions need not form an orthonormal inverse under inherited scale/shear.

Keep front-particle filtering and sampled chest contact first. Each capsule reads current x/y/z after any earlier contact has changed them; never reuse a particle projection across ordered collisions. Preserve capsule and particle order, eight constraint iterations, fixed60Hz/.05s/three-step catch-up,60-step returning visibility preparation, pause/hidden handling, reset/teleport detection, mesh publication and teardown. Rebuild bounds inside every step rather than infer unchanged pose from public stationarity. No cross-step revision/cache, new scene API or GPU resource exists.

The new path still pays all three relative-coordinate subtractions, all three current axis projections, finite checks and up to six bound comparisons for every eligible pair. ### First-use route preparation

PF16.84 is retained after exact source/static, actual-worker/held-lifecycle/failure and real-HTTP caller qualification. It follows measured first-use route construction through the existing worker warmup, without changing time policy. Corrected queries show235–245ms body-specific construction after runtime/map preparation has already finished, overlapping the longest idle-to-motion wait after its receipt. Cost grows with map changes, including maps with no later query; no new per-tick scan, worker or navigation authority. Require supported source selection plus actual lifecycle/current-body/map/cancel/load/failure/shutdown checks and a matched cold-route latency gain before retention. The accepted private plan prefers the eligible actor named by the saved controlled-role binding, with the first eligible ordered world actor as fallback, only when the existing map/timeline key changes. Use the exact body reader and plain profile values; pass at most one optional warm body, and do not displace a real request’s existing body preparation. The role is an engine-owned saved binding rather than a human dimension or local-auth-only service accessor. Temporary Sleep/guard or other command restrictions do not disqualify the designated body: current geometry is selected through existing alive/physical participation, support, flight/fall and body readers, without inventing a new walking-kind API. Warmup failure must remain non-authoritative diagnostic work without poisoning the valid map, swallowing actual query/map errors or leaking failed mesh resources. The pinned generator already frees success:false allocations; the fourth navigation owner needs only successful-mesh cleanup/rethrow if query-wrapper creation fails before cache ownership. Prepare privately under the40–90-line estimate, obtain source/static review, then serialize actual-worker cold/warm/current-body/cancel/map/load/death/failure/shutdown checks and latency evidence after PF16.79 pair and lifecycle disposition. The qualified four-owner change is selected for canonical integration. It shifts roughly191ms into earlier map preparation rather than removing that work; the four fresh-worker cases preserve identical routes. A fresh real-HTTP pair on the same initial grounded body/geometry reduces command-to-first-authoritative1m movement352.504→127.205ms, with real route-worker215.799→10.952ms, after initial map preparation is awaited outside input. This is one first-use pair, not visible browser motion or a net startup/CPU/capacity guarantee. Temporary sleep can spend preparation on a body that never moves, and a different request arriving during an already-running speculative build may wait for it; already queued real requests keep dispatch priority. The initial requirement of no additional startup cost is explicitly superseded by the accepted bounded tradeoff: about191ms more worker-preparation time and140ms more map-ready time in the one actual HTTP pair, for225ms less first authoritative movement delay. Cache/worker bounds remain unchanged, with no total CPU/startup claim. This preparation experiment kept the original clock; the later PF16.82 decision separately retains elapsed debt. ### Complete action menus when the player asks

PF16.62 is selected for complete implementation after the combined graphical gate fails: 2.12438× actual server progress, draw p99/max 50.25/96.6 ms, accepted-command receipt p95 693 ms, and stationary first displayed movement p95/max 1,224.8 ms. The private full-view attribution spends 4.712 of 5.964 seconds in action sections (79.0%, including some retained cheap controls), with 47,005 nested native previews. This is material evidence for the complete cross-layer change below. The coherent first implementation is reassessed at roughly 950–1,250 changed logic lines including deletion, rather than the initial 700–1,000 estimate: explicit consent/work controls, exact held-item use and the diagnostic survival caller all need complete current contracts. This remains the same bounded feature scope; no new cache, endpoint or saved representation is added. Canonical SQL/world/cape code, not a disabled-view diagnostic, supplies the graphical baseline. Preserve the complete feature path during integration and qualify the result before retention. This implementation is now retained: selected static/focused/native/HTTP and actual-browser lifecycle checks pass, including immediate death/aim/agreement controls. The matched expanded mixed-travel graphical pair improves1.84162→2.59002×, with all16real meals/return activity preserved; sustained smooth3×, ordinary rest and aging remain open. [Complete current evidence](../verification/performance/01-interaction-and-early-server.md#requested-action-menu-functional-qualification).

The selected policy reuses the authenticated `/api/actions` owner. Opening the existing searchable picker prepares complete current choices for the selected person, object, possession or known recipe; the full catalogue remains available explicitly. Add the current learned-activity and scar-treatment choices to that owner before removing their continuous-view versions. Keep explicit paged inventory controls, inspection disclosure, split/drop quantities, equipment and learned-record actions. Routine updates retain the facts needed to identify possessions and methods, current work, Stop, recovery, death, aiming and the ending of trade/outing/other consent; they no longer prepare every possible action. This is one current contract change across protocol, HTTP validation, projection, catalogue, inventory projection and every UI/diagnostic consumer, not empty legacy lists or a second offer cache.

The deliberate feature costs are removal of automatic suggestions, the three saved quick-action pins and their 1–3 keyboard activation. Players open an action picker instead. Known-recipe descriptions and ingredient facts stay available, while current Craft eligibility is checked when that recipe's picker opens. The T equipped-item gesture remains: select the canonical preferred held-item identity through its existing world owner, request choices for that exact item and clicked target, then use the ordinary command path only while scope, item, target and the active targeting gesture still match. That introduces one read before execution and removes advance action-validity highlighting; neutral public target highlighting remains. Escape, replaced targets, lost equipment or control and restored timelines invalidate pending choices without retrying them.

Cost currently grows with viewers × updates × visible targets × usable tools/known recipes; the browser also rebuilds quick-action rows, run closures and editable-picker option data from every delivered list. Preparation becomes request-scoped at the existing catalogue and inventory owners. Possession, body/status, known methods, target visibility/position, definitions, consent, control, pause and timeline can change eligibility, so fetched choices never authorize later execution and must be discarded when their viewing/request identity is obsolete. No new endpoint, shared cache, saved-world representation, compatibility reader or spending is planned.

Implementation order is: (1) attribute current complete-view action cost and record whether the headroom justifies the UI loss; (2) complete self/item/target/recipe catalogue contexts and preferred-equipment selection; (3) replace continuous offer construction and all dependent consumers together, including the survival driver; (4) update current UI/action documentation and the relevant limits inventory; (5) run focused equivalence and failure checks, actual UI interaction/visual checks and matched complete-view/browser measurements. Coordinate canonical file ownership before step 2 so the memory-write work does not overlap. Scope spans `apps/server/src/{view,action-catalogue,inventory-view,http}.ts`, protocol DTOs, the existing world equipped-action owner, client main/pickers/panels/inventory/death/work controls and the existing browser driver; reassess before extending beyond that set.

Required verification covers unchanged permitted non-action facts and unchanged world/event/random results from reads; complete self, target, item, recipe, learned-activity and treatment choices under installed definitions; Stop for queued/paused work, recovery/death, current aiming and consent endings; explicit inventory inspection, quantities, equipment, learning and durable receipt recovery; actual picker search/keyboard/focus/error behavior; and held replies across target/equipment/control/pause/privacy/timeline changes. Update every current wire caller together and keep native admission as authority. Use the relevant existing checks, TypeScript/config/production build, bounded private scenarios and actual browser screenshots. Retention requires material complete-path benefit with preserved current-format/privacy behavior and honestly reported feature costs; neither helper speed nor a viewer-free run completes the expanded graphical/sustained 3× gate.

Cost grows with living participants × simulated intervals × physical/sensory pairs, plus viewers × prepared action choices and rendered frames × animated geometry. Share immutable rules, exact unchanged geometry, required action preparation and owned numeric buffers. Geometry, stance, custody, knowledge, access, timeline and timed eligibility invalidate the corresponding reuse. Full completion is actual 3× with the expanded integrated population and verified everyday work/travel/rest, responsive player input, frame tails without long stalls, and no silent storage failure. A faster isolated helper, absent viewer or disabled mechanics is a diagnostic result, never full completion. Preserve privacy, command integrity, current-format save/load and real spending/accounting through all trials.

Expected initial scope is 500–1,500 logic lines across existing profiling tools and measured runtime owners, reassessed after attribution. This is cross-layer work with material correctness and measurement risk. Existing gameplay, privacy, deterministic effects, saved randomness, current-format recovery and durable command acknowledgement remain requirements. The owner permits considered gameplay compromises, but each consequential change must be justified by evidence and recorded before implementation; arbitrary population, perception or action caps are not a default solution.

The first forty-five minutes identify repeated save round trips, discarded action-preview mutations, short-lived brief-sighting predictions, sequential authority reads and first-use shader stalls. The initial reassessment was roughly 450–650 runtime logic lines plus 1,500–2,200 lines of opt-in profiling/workload support. Consumer/privacy integration and further measured owner trials now put the expected changed runtime logic around 1,000–1,400 lines and diagnostic support around 2,100–2,800 lines, excluding tests and prose. The larger diagnostic portion adds whole-run frame distributions, movement observations after idle inputs, actual everyday routines and failure attribution; it does not add a general benchmarking service. PF16 in the existing performance tracker owns each pre-registered experiment and its retention decision. The early prediction-cache timing was inconclusive; later matched busy traces and CPU evidence govern retention rather than correctness alone. Final diagnostic reassessment is approximately 2,500–3,200 changed logic lines across the existing scene drivers, finite workload and bounded attribution helpers, excluding tests and prose; runtime scope remains approximately 1,000–1,400 logic lines. The added diagnostic scope covers sustained real input, late callback gaps, source/clock provenance and observed failures rather than another general benchmarking system.

PF16.46 completes the read-only repeated-publication audit without a runtime trial. The current diagnostic driver awaits each resident's durable job, native admission and terminal diagnostic sequentially, so it supplies no already-queued resident burst to group. Generic batching would require explicit per-operation authority fences and receipts, refusal/rollback isolation and shared publication accounting across the service/store owners; the current commit contract carries one ordinary receipt and authority fence. PF16.47 prepares a minimal private separate-process timing probe before the next complete scene capture. The existing trivial-query probe shares the game's Node callback thread, so an elapsed database request can include a blocked game callback. One independent read-only query per second and one bounded callback-lag aggregate distinguish that condition from delay also visible outside the game process. The probe uses only the owned disposable database, closes all resources and reports its extra instrumentation, exact clock boundaries, errors and source identity; it changes no runtime behavior and cannot itself qualify game capacity. It completes 1,780 queries without error, with 1,750.303 seconds of game overlap and an uncovered 49.708-second final tail. During the exact 55.724-second browser gap, 55 independent queries still finish with median 1.627 ms/maximum 116.534 ms. This rules against a database-wide outage lasting that full interval but does not identify the browser stall or the discarded maintenance rejection.

PF16.51 preregisters one twelve-minute coarse browser CPU diagnostic after PF16.49 and cleanup. Exact sampled/measured overlap and timestamped callback gaps govern attribution; idle/native or missing samples cannot name a JavaScript cause. The standing-work fixture, application, controls, failure rules and canonical profiler limits remain unchanged, and this diagnostic cannot qualify capacity or mixed NPC travel. 1. Read the complete performance contract/tracker/profiling guidance and applicable subsystem guidance. Inventory existing optimizations and current measurements before choosing interventions.
2. Capture current native CPU and full PostgreSQL server baselines, then the actual built browser. Separate frame intervals/long tasks, click feedback, durable acknowledgement, first confirmed movement, navigation preparation, native progress/debt, publication, SQL/queue waits and retained memory. Keep raw profiles private and matched inputs/machine/build explicit.
3. Extend existing tools only for missing measurements and workload. Exercise the integrated starting content plus ten additional people, more animals/material objects and buildings. Verify actual populations and native everyday activity rather than assuming requested counts or fixture model quality.
4. Record every candidate as an unchecked experiment in the existing performance tracker before attempting it. Remove repeated work through its semantic owner first; compare matched runs and ordered native outcomes. Retain useful architectural simplification, reject neutral/regressive experiments, and record results for both.
5. Reprofile against the dominant remaining cost. Include cold preparation, invalidation, stopping/replacing walking, geometry/content edits and current-format restart where affected. Keep costly timing runs sequential so agents do not manufacture contention.
6. Run focused existing checks/ad-hoc downstream scenarios, changed-file formatting, TypeScript and production build; inspect the complete diff and resolve in-scope findings. Checkpoint locally at least every fifteen minutes of edits. Reconcile behavior, measurements, trackers and remaining limits before final commit/handoff.

Cost grows independently with active people/animals, nearby density, world roots/buildings, simulation boundaries, action/event rate, observers, retained evidence and publications. Share unchanged definitions, geometry and phase/request preparation at their existing owners. Any retained reuse must name exact invalidators, including mutable drafts, pose/sense/geometry/definition changes, participant loss and restore. Measure complete production callers; isolated helper wins do not qualify the game.

## Target and owners

The October 15 renewed task uses the actual current complete opening plus the requested ten additional people, twenty additional animals, one hundred other roots and three diagnostic sheds: seventeen people including the player, forty animals and 133 other physical roots overall. All sixteen NPC people must perform ordinary work, approach travel, produced-food meals and rest through real owners. The separate unexpanded correctness fixture pads the same authored opening to twenty NPC people, twenty-one animals and one hundred other roots; its twenty-one people are not the population of the authored opening. Neither fixture removes authored starting content.

The historical September target was one player, twenty NPC agents, twenty animals including the seeded flying bird, and one hundred visible objects. Broader population qualification remains with PF11. For the renewed expanded task, measure actual 3× progress (180 game seconds per real second), command/publication tails, event-loop stalls, browser frames, SQL work and retained memory. Include cold exposure and a warmed scene; throughput alone does not establish smooth play. No absolute zero-stutter claim follows from finite samples.

Continue PF00/PF03/PF05/PF08/PF09 and PF12.3–PF12.8 under [performance](../maintainers/performance.md), [elapsed time](../simulation-time.md) and [perception/reactions](../events-perception-and-reactions.md). Canonical state/commit ownership stays in WorldService/repositories; cognition uses ActorWork, and presentation consumes permitted deltas. Limits remain in their existing inventories.

## Sequence and candidate work

1. Attribute the live small-world symptom without mutating it. Build one bounded, disposable full-server scenario reusing the existing population builder, metrics and CPU profiler. Compare PostgreSQL with SQLite/native to separate SQL round trips from simulation CPU. Include zero-cost cognition admission, SSE, repeated native actions and actual timer progress.
2. Profile native motion/perception, repeated validation/draft work, SQL writes/reads, cognition wakeups and public projection. Audit bird event/awareness growth and unchanged object work. Rank measured hotspots; retain baseline reports outside Git.
3. Implement the smallest coherent optimizations, repeating matched workloads after each meaningful group. Reuse immutable dependency-valid results and coalesce replacement state; retain action fences, meaningful evidence and explicit-command durability. Consider presentation-only quality/cadence separately from authoritative simulation. Do not add arbitrary salience mechanics unless measurements demonstrate a missing consumed policy and its world owner is explicit.
4. Exercise affected lifecycle/failure cases: movement cancellation, observation loss/wake, stale control, restart and failed database writes. Inspect carried-over branch findings against current owners; do not claim unmerged revision/follow mechanics were tested.
5. Inspect and interact with the loaded browser scene; measure frame/update behavior separately. Review the full final diff, fix notable findings, and reconcile trackers/limits/evidence. Run relevant typecheck/build/format/config checks once at the end; no unit/integration/browser suites.

## Verification and constraints

Use fresh disposable data, separate localhost ports, explicit empty provider credentials and a fixture transport with zero spending. Never reset the player's current world. Use bounded runs and terminate only owned processes. Disk has about 31 GiB free at baseline; keep database/profile artifacts small. Preserve one lockfile and existing dependencies. Reuse diagnostic scripts rather than assembling a new benchmark framework.

Completion: measured bottlenecks addressed, representative 3× full-server/browser scene exercised, actual before/after results and remaining limits reported, changed behavior checked through downstream callers, documentation and final review complete. Broader hosted/population/GPU and paid-model quality acceptance remain separate.

### Findings and scope refinements

The original bird concern was repeated observation, not flight-position storage. The initial change lowered routine native-animal sighting importance while retaining the then-current records. The subsequently accepted world-owned sighting policy now omits individual routine animal sighting records and automatic reasoning while preserving current visibility, interest matches and meaningful events ([current follow-through](../maintainers/performance.md#current-3-local-scene-follow-through)). Do not recreate the superseded storage/trigger policy from this historical implementation sequence; no general per-object salience editor was introduced.

Review rejected the tentative middle status-reconciliation skip: its measured gain was within noise and did not justify another zero-time mutation assumption. Existing shared entity roots/spatial/limited-query owners remain; finer changed-target visibility reuse is still PF09/PF12.3. Added coalescing and a bounded diagnostic backlog because inspector snapshots were repeatedly queued while newer versions superseded them; reliable accounting remains independent.

The requested sustained stall-free target is **not yet accepted**. Earlier scene runs retained command/publication tails despite approximately 3× progress. Earlier base and candidate runs both collapsed under a host load above 230 on 10 logical CPUs. Later completed PostgreSQL runs sustain nearly 3×, but still contain cold and warm gaps. Preserve both sets of evidence and keep the target open.

### Continued investigation after native-action merge

User requested continued implementation. Refreshed `origin/main` (`a39311dc`); local `main` and the isolated branch already contain the newer native-action merge `75ee15f7`. Preserve that merge and both copies of the existing changes. Continue under PF01/PF09/PF12 with an estimated 100–300 additional logic lines, subject to measured attribution:

1. Inspect memory eligibility/embedding-queue SQL plans and statement amplification under a fresh disposable database. Compare work counts as well as latency; the overloaded host cannot establish capacity acceptance.
2. Inspect merged perception and native-action callers for repeated visibility/context work; reuse the current semantic owner and prove invalidation before retaining an optimization.

4. Review the complete resulting diff, update actual evidence and outstanding acceptance, run final static checks once, and deliver changes to the normal checkout. No automated suites, paid inference, or changes to the active player save.

The continued PostgreSQL run reproduced a background scheduling storage failure that paused the entire world; later tracing also found ordinary write timeouts. Narrow this behavior: an explicitly read-only maintenance scheduling failure defers maintenance for 60 seconds and emits a failed diagnostic; authoritative commit/dispatch failures retain their existing fences and pause behavior. No automatic paid retry or memory deletion is introduced. Skip maintenance metadata reads when neither cleanup, dream review nor reflection can run. This is a consequential failure-isolation change within the authorized performance scope.

The 30-second merged PostgreSQL profile counted 1,294 advisory status checks plus repeated world-head reads. The follow-through removes the separate header read (the result is advisory and ActorWork/current-source selection retain generation fences) and coalesces each actor’s inspections to at most once per real second through existing ActorWork deferral. Unchanged actors remain asleep until their simulation deadline or a real change; this is not periodic polling of every actor.

Continued runtime verification completed: PostgreSQL batch equivalence/rollback, merged native follow/death/locomotion and sleep/wake behavior, HTTP/SQLite rollback with a pending follow action, scheduling-read failure/recovery, and 30/60-second full-server PostgreSQL runs. The last achieved 2.998× with requested endpoint populations; cold and warm stalls remain explicitly unaccepted. Static/review results are recorded in the evidence owner.

## Hearing integration and update latency — September 27

Final browser follow-through adds roughly 90 logic lines for the observed HUD overlap: React measures fixed controls and open panels only when their size, viewport, scale, opening or animation changes; the existing caption placer treats those rectangles as occupied, tries its neutral fallback and pauses reading when no position fits. This supports a compact batch-encoding experiment, not a guaranteed saving or production change. [Complete evidence](../verification/performance/51-current-database-time.md).
